Read `AGENTS.md`, `docs/development-workflow.md`, `docs/function-type-migration-plan.md` (Progress section), `docs/milestones/100-call-spread.md` and `docs/milestones/callable-regression-repairs.md` first.

## Standing instructions from the user

- One agent works in this tree at a time. There is no git; snapshot files into `archive/` before a risky phase.
- Implementation first; use focused TDD and quick bounded compiler/native/editor checks. Full regressions and timing wait for a completed feature-family milestone, including compiler, LSP and ready benchmark support. The former five-gate/checkpoint101 schedule is superseded. Do not resume its stopped continuation by default. Use a separate `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` when another cargo job holds the lock.
- Fix defects when found, in the same pass, with a regression test. Do not only record them.
- New built-in types need clear IntelliSense in `crates/tsrust-lsp/src/builtins.json`.

## Gates 102 to 112 (later the same day)

Each gate followed the same order: failing compiler test, implementation, catalog documentation with native-verified examples, editor test, matched benchmark pair with independent oracle, untimed native and compiled-Node verification, milestone record. No timing was run.

- 102 `Array.from`: array copy, optional value/index mapping, string characters. [Milestone](milestones/102-array-from.md).
- 103 `array.keys()` and `array.values()`: ordinary independent arrays, no iterator object. [Milestone](milestones/103-array-keys-values.md).
- 104 `String.at`: Unicode character position from either end, `string | undefined`. [Milestone](milestones/104-string-at.md).
- 105 `Number.parseInt`: radix 2 through 36, Node-checked. [Milestone](milestones/105-number-parse-int.md).
- 106 `number.toString(radix?)`: output identical to Node on the reference fixtures. [Milestone](milestones/106-number-to-string.md).
- 107 `Object.is`: same-value comparison; arrays and records by storage identity. [Milestone](milestones/107-object-is.md).
- 108 `Math.f16round`: half-precision rounding, Node-checked. [Milestone](milestones/108-math-f16round.md).
- 109 `Math.random`: uniform number in [0, 1), new sequence per run. [Milestone](milestones/109-math-random.md).
- 110 `String.fromCodePoint` and `String.fromCharCode`: int codes with spread arrays; lone surrogates become U+FFFD. [Milestone](milestones/110-string-from-codes.md).
- 111 `string.codePointAt` and `string.charCodeAt`: codes by character position; `int | undefined` and `number` results. [Milestone](milestones/111-string-code-at.md).
- 112 `string.toLocaleLowerCase` and `string.toLocaleUpperCase`: locale-independent mapping, no locale argument. [Milestone](milestones/112-string-locale-case.md).

Defects fixed in passing: `number[].includes(NaN)` returned `false`; the editor test for `Array.of` had a stale example count. Gate 108 fixed two more: number text took the wrong digit when two shortest strings were equally near, and numbers in template literals printed `inf`, `-0` and no exponent form. The editor now shows `number` for every `Math` call and constant.

Last focused results: all 162 editor tests and all 306 benchmark harness tests pass; the focused compiler suites named in each milestone pass. A complete compiler regression was not run.

Array family status: only `entries()` (needs index/value tuples) and the iterator symbol remain. Math is complete except the `Symbol.toStringTag` property. String candidates next: `localeCompare` and the regular-expression methods `match`, `matchAll` and `search`. Numeric `toLocaleString` is accepted at gate 114. Object functions beyond `Object.is` need a design for record keys and entries.

## Gate 117: fixed positional tuple types

Fixed owning data tuples are accepted with contextual literals, static positional indices, length, replacement, cloning, owned position transfer/restoration, nested and nullable positions, tuple arrays, tuple-returning map callbacks and structural generic/imported identities. Native display and Array.isArray preserve array behavior; project facts retain nominal member identity inside positional tuple metadata. See [milestone 117](milestones/117-tuple-types.md).

Eleven focused tuple tests have passing evidence across retained runs, including the normal-stack depth-100 native witness and imported nominal tuple facts. Adjacent array_join (4), array_flat (4), nested_records (14) and three generic/substitution unit groups (3 each) pass. Six compiler fixtures match Node independently. Three editor tests pass, including positional signatures and the executable tuple length example. The benchmark pair passes five untimed native/Node-after-tsc cases. All 316 harness tests have passing evidence across 315 initial passes and the 18 affected report/registration supplements. Full regressions and timing remain deferred; SQLite measurement records are unchanged. Snapshots remain in `archive/tuple-types-start`.

Computed tuple indices, named aliases, labelled/optional/rest/callable positions and variable-length tuple methods remain unsupported. Flattening tuple positions is rejected rather than silently retaining their array-shaped values; valid flat depths that only remove ordinary array dimensions remain supported. Positional editor inference and the report category registration defects found during acceptance are repaired.

Next gate is 118: Array.entries. Reuse the tuple foundation for typed index/value pairs, ownership-safe copying, complete editor documentation and matched untimed benchmark support. Preserve the existing eager-array convention of keys()/values() and describe supported iteration behavior precisely. Continue gates until the user changes direction or a decision needs feedback.

See [milestone 118](milestones/118-array-entries.md).

Nine focused compiler tests pass across the retained eight-test acceptance and recursive supplement. Adjacent keys/values (2) and tuple types (11) pass. Acceptance also repairs tuple selection after generic receiver resolution without evaluating receivers twice, fixed record-array copying, and nullable tuple-array editor members. Preserve the original red and failed checks together with accepted supplements.

Next gate is 119. Investigate record enumeration foundations for Object.keys/values/entries, including constructor property order and heterogeneous value representation. Do not substitute declaration order for observable JavaScript property order. Continue gates until the user changes direction or a decision needs feedback.

## Gate 119: Object.keys

Object.keys is accepted for fixed source records, tuples, arrays, strings, numbers and booleans. Records retain each value's constructor field order, rather than declaration order. Field replacement, explicit cloning, recursive graph cloning and fixed-record conversion retain order. Arrays and tuples return decimal positional names. Strings enumerate UTF-16 code-unit positions; numbers/booleans return empty arrays. Null, undefined, nullable receivers and functions are rejected. Dynamic additions, deletion, prototype/symbol properties and numeric/quoted source record keys remain unsupported. See [milestone 119](milestones/119-object-keys.md).

Six compiler tests pass, with adjacent Array.entries (9), tuple_types (11) and nested_records (14). Four fixtures match unadapted Node Object.keys; explicit native clone() uses structuredClone in the fixture only. Two editor tests pass, including both native documentation examples. The benchmark pair passes five untimed native/Node-after-tsc cases. All 320 harness checks have passing evidence across 319 original passes and 18 affected registration/report supplements. The original harness uses Node v26.3.0 from login-shell selection; the Node fixture, benchmark and affected supplement use the pinned v24.11.1 runtime. Preserve these identities and the rejected initial benchmark launch. No full regressions, timing or allocation profiling; SQLite measurements unchanged. Baseline: archive/object-keys-start.

Only record types actually observed by Object.keys receive order metadata, discovered across checked functions, closures and specialized closure methods. Unobserved types retain their prior native layouts. The analyzer lives in a dedicated helper to keep recursive dispatch small. All three compiler/editor consumers are rebuilt and refreshed. The harness found a missing Other category registration; that registration is repaired and passed the affected supplement.

Next gate is 120. Investigate heterogeneous data unions/array leaves for Object.values and Object.entries. Homogeneous-only enumeration is not a complete solution for mixed record fields. Preserve order metadata and all completed acceptance evidence. Continue gates until the user changes direction or a decision needs feedback.

Arrays accept a declared union element type at any rank. Both functions copy record fields in constructor order. Mixed field types give a union element type. Tuples are supported; `Object.values` also copies arrays. Two fixtures match Node. ## Gate 123: record display order and union strict equality

Console display of a record follows its constructor order (owner decision, for consistency with the Object functions). `===` and `!==` compare a union with an int, number, boolean or string, or with another primitive union. An untyped array literal with mixed value types takes a union element type. [Milestone](milestones/123-record-display-order-and-union-equality.md).

Last focused results: 182 editor tests and 326 harness checks pass.

## Gate 124: destructuring and borrowed const bindings

Array and record patterns work in `const`, `let` and `for...of`, with nesting, skips and renames. Names follow the access of the source; nothing is cloned (owner decision). A `const` taken from a loop element or a `ReadOnly` parameter is a readonly borrow. The parser lowers patterns to ordinary declarations, so no other pass changed. Guide: [Destructuring](destructuring.md). [Milestone](milestones/124-destructuring.md).

Last focused results: 183 editor tests and 328 harness checks pass.

## Gate 125: parameter patterns and indexed borrows

Function and arrow parameters accept patterns; without a stated access their names borrow the argument. A `const` taken from an indexed element of a named array borrows it, so `const first = words[0];` and `const [a, b] = words;` work for owned elements. A stack overflow on type annotations nested past the limit is fixed (it predates gate 121). `value === "yes"` narrows a union variable on the equal path. The missing-backend acceptance test is repaired (the backend was also found under the Cargo home). [Milestone](milestones/125-parameter-patterns-and-indexed-borrows.md).

Last focused results: 184 editor tests, 328 harness checks and every compiler suite named in the milestone pass, including the full `acceptance` suite.

Open in this area: rest elements and default values in patterns. Borrows are lexical: the source stays loaned until the borrowing const leaves scope.

Next candidates: `Object.fromEntries` and `Object.assign` need a design for building records from dynamic names; `Object.hasOwn`, `Object.freeze`/`isFrozen` and `Object.groupBy` are smaller. Strict equality between union values, untyped mixed array literals and destructuring remain open in the type system. Continue gates until the user changes direction or a decision needs feedback.

## Gate 127 accepted: destructuring defaults

Defaults now work in declaration, parameter/callback and for...of patterns, including nesting, renames, whole parameter defaults, missing array/tuple positions and absent fixed-record fields. Selection is undefined-only and lazy; null is preserved. Source expressions run once. Names follow source access without implicit cloning, and fresh borrowed fallback owners persist in local storage. Source loans remain lexical.

A late review found parameter defaults were initialized before body-lowered patterns. Patterns now initialize left to right alongside their parameter, so later defaults can reference earlier destructured names and later names are unavailable. Nested arrow defaults retain separate parser pattern state. Nullable generic default shapes and fallback union owner annotations are repaired too. [Guide](destructuring.md). [Milestone](milestones/127-destructuring-defaults.md).

## Owner policy: use built-in testing assertions

Use std:testing in executable fixtures wherever possible instead of logging values for stdout comparisons. Implement missing assertion/matcher APIs with TDD when a needed check requires them; do not weaken tests or fall back to logging. Keep console-output assertions for actual printing behavior and independent Node references. Prefer borrowing assertions and preserve ownership; no implicit clone. This is recorded in docs/development-workflow.md.

All16 default checks now use assertions for native execution through the module CLI, with static rejection witnesses unchanged. Seven fixtures have empty stdout and passing real Node assertion counterparts; original logged Node fixtures are retained in archive/destructuring-defaults-assertions-start. Migration found and repaired project_link.rs name qualification for earlier pattern names in later parameter defaults, for functions and arrows. Optional_parameters21, project75 and three editor checks pass. Current debug/editor/release consumers are refreshed; five matched defaults cases pass untimed (2026-10-05T09-59-34-614Z-verify.json). Gate127 remains accepted. No full regressions or timing.

### Owner decisions: explicit spread copying and mixed default sorting

The owner authorizes copying when explicitly requested with spread/rest syntax, including `let clonedThing = { ...thing }`. Ordinary bindings and destructuring accesses still must not introduce implicit clones. Rest/spread copying remains implementation work, not an accepted feature.

Mixed-alternative default sorting remains unsupported to preserve type-aware language choices. Use an explicit comparator when ordering distinct alternatives. Do not silently introduce JavaScript text ordering or change accepted homogeneous default ordering.

### Gate 128 accepted: Object.hasOwn

### Gate 137 accepted: global number names

See docs/milestones/137-global-number-names.md and docs/global-number-names.md. `parseInt`, `parseFloat`, `isNaN`, `isFinite`, `NaN` and `Infinity` are rewritten during analysis to their `Number` members when no declaration has the name (Analyzer::global_number_name; syntax::global_number_member). The project linker, the generic-default scope check, the name and call type hints and `typeof` know the names too. A new global name must be added in all of those places: the linker otherwise renames an undeclared name to a module symbol as soon as the program has an import.

### Gate 138 accepted: `let` without an initializer

See docs/milestones/138-deferred-initialization.md and docs/deferred-initialization.md. The parser gives `let name: Type;` a placeholder initializer (Expr::deferred / Expr::is_deferred: an `undefined` node with an empty span), so StmtKind::Let is unchanged. Analyzer::deferred_let binds the name without a value; definite_assignment.rs checks uses after analysis (called from analyze and analyze_recursive); emit writes `let mut valueN: Type;`. Any new pass that reads a Let initializer must treat a deferred placeholder as "no value".

Function-typed deferred variables are rejected. Loop bodies never count as assigning for the code after the loop.

### Owner clarification: runtime failures stay within Tsrust

The owner clarified that Tsrust should automate safe code writing and ideally prevent native panics or unrecoverable native failures outside Tsrust. Use recoverable Tsrust errors for ordinary supported-operation failures, including existing bounds/overflow failures. The previous recommendation to keep those permanently fatal is superseded. This resolves the recovery-direction question; do not ask it again. Error types/backend/effect and cleanup details still need implementation. This is a target, not a current guarantee; external process termination cannot be promised recoverable. docs/error-handling.md and the design draft record the distinction. No compiler source changed by this clarification; gate142 remains unaccepted.

### Gate 142 source work resumed: exception AST and async room

The owner approved continuing with full try/catch/finally (also try/finally) and explicitly asked to preserve room for advanced async implementation. Recovery direction is resolved; do not ask again. Keep error effects separate from execution and retain finally/pending completion state so later suspension/cancellation can be added. Async behavior itself is not implemented by this gate.

Initial parser red accidentally retained the std:testing import; the corrected red strips the project import line and targets the actual missing exception syntax. AST integration compile failures are retained and repaired.

Shared walkers, project linking, generic-default scopes, checked projections and conservative fixed-storage fallback know the new nodes. Catch binding scopes are distinct. Analyzer block checking and callable-result discovery deliberately return unsupported while exception semantics are pending; definite assignment has the same guard, and emission assumes unchecked exceptions never reach it. Do not mistake parser acceptance for language acceptance. One unused CatchClause.binding warning is expected until analysis populates it. Existing installed consumers still represent gate141; no refresh or benchmark work yet.

Next: error type/effect inference and completion flow, with recoverable native operation errors; preserve source return/break/continue targets through finally and all owner/assignment states. ### Gate 142 in progress: matcher witness repaired, parser stack repaired

Snapshots: `archive/exception-opaque-source-diagnosis-start` (analyzer, effects, storage registry, this record) and `archive/exception-parser-stack-start` (parser).

Cause of the matcher rejection: `expect(1).toBe(2)` makes the first analysis fail with `recursion-inference`, so `analyze_recursive` runs speculative access passes with `effects_only` set. Those passes did not register stored-callable origins, so every storage family had no origin, every stored call (`compare(expected)` inside the matchers) was opaque, and the catch contract was rejected inside the speculative pass. The factory and the native bindings were never the opaque source. Fix: the analyzer has a `speculative_access_run` flag, set only by `analyze_recursive` for its discarded passes; in such a pass `Registry::note_speculative_origin` records the origin for effect lookup without layout validation. A temporary effects-only scope inside a definitive run does not set the flag, so the unit test `discovery_and_effect_passes_do_not_publish_storage_origins` still holds. `/private/tmp/exception-speculative-origin-repair.log`: exception_handling 20/20.

Two more defects were found by the affected batch and repaired:

- `closures::closure_nesting_and_malformed_bodies_are_bounded` overflowed the 2 MB test stack before this repair too (confirmed on the snapshot sources). `Parser::statement` had a 14.6 KB debug frame. Named declarations and all other statement forms now parse in `named_declaration` and `other_statement`, and the arrow-function parser is split into `closure_signature`, `closure_body` and `closure_expression`. The test now needs less than 1.6 MB. Measure frames with lldb and the script pattern in the session scratchpad if this regresses; keep new statement forms out of `statement`.
- `error_effects::tests::lexical_native_path_origins_have_resolved_pure_contracts` still expected every testing-runtime binding except `checkAssertion` to be unresolved. It now lists the audited bindings and requires every other binding to stay unresolved.

## Gate 143 accepted — checked type assertions (2026-10-06)

Owner approved assignability-only `as` and source-preserving `satisfies`.
Implementation and acceptance are complete. `as` supplies compatible context and
explicit scalar integer-to-number conversion. `satisfies` retains the source type,
with required context for empty arrays, record literals and callable expressions.
Unsafe narrowing is rejected with `typeof` advice. Ownership and lexical borrows
are preserved; assertions cannot clone or turn a borrowed value into an owner.

Repairs during TDD: numeric widening needed real native conversion; record/callable
literals needed the existing contextual resolver; comparison `<` after a primitive
annotation was consumed as a generic application; assertion wrappers lost borrowed
initializer detection, added duplicate native references/views, and lost integer
power inference. Positive native cases now cover these paths. Boxed annotations
are visited by source substitution, record materialization and project linking.
The helper stays outside the shared recursive analyzer frame.

tests across the gate; focused editor 4/4; harness 369/369 with the ABI registration

## Gate 144: nominal enums accepted

with automatic numeric values, constant arithmetic/bitwise initializers, earlier
member references, copied values, generics, arrays, record fields, unions and
exported aliases. Enums are distinct types; primitive or cross-enum assignment is
rejected. Explicit Number/String conversion and typeof follow backing values.
Static namespace only: no reverse lookup, mutable runtime object, heterogeneous
enums, runtime initializers or const enum.

## Gate 145: Set implementation in progress (not accepted)

Gate 144 remains the latest accepted gate. Gate 145 introduces typed Set keys
(int/number/boolean/string/null/undefined/enums and their unions), construction
from arrays/sets/strings or empty/null/undefined sources, size, add/has/delete/
clear, keys/values snapshots and borrowed for-of. add returns void; snapshots
return arrays. Explicit construction/snapshots copy keys; ordinary access and
bindings do not clone. Runtime hash buckets hold insertion-slot indices, so
storage does not duplicate owned keys. NaN and signed zeros use SameValueZero.
See draft docs/sets.md. Set uses Type::Set(id), ArrayElement::Set(id,depth), and
Record.set_definition metadata, following the enum named-type plumbing.

## Gate 155 accepted: Unicode string for-of

String loops visit Unicode scalar values; characters are readonly string borrows.
Source expression runs once and its lexical loan ends at loop exit. Mutation,
replacement, character transfer and let-character reassignment are rejected;
explicit clone(character) works. Normal break/continue/throw/finally work. Native
length remains UTF-8 bytes. The existing Cow<'static,str> ABI cannot represent
borrowed dynamic character substrings; the dedicated inline-never emitter creates
one owning character string per iteration, lends it to the body, then drops it.
No source clone or whole-array snapshot. Editor character hover/methods work.
